What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm just... having trouble keeping things straight at work. I work at a dry cleaner's, and... well, everything's just a mess lately. The tickets, the receipts, the notes from the workshop, the customer orders... they just pile up. I look at it all and I just... I don't know what to do first anymore. It's getting too hard to manage.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble focusing on tasks at work or getting easily distracted?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess so. I'll start going over the day's orders... and it's fine at first. But after a few minutes, my mind just... wanders off. I'll start thinking about choir plans, or... just things at home. And then I look down and realize I missed half of what I was supposed to check.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about things more than you think you should?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think so. I mean, I get stressed when the tickets pile up, but... I don't really just sit around worrying about things. It's mostly just... what I said before.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches, stomachaches, or trembling?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't really noticed anything like that. Just... normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I don't think I feel... on edge or anything. Just normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I'm not really sad. Just... busy, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations at work?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I just go in and do my job at the cleaners. I don't really avoid anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I get tired after a long shift, but... just the normal amount, I guess. Nothing unusual.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I just... I just do what needs doing. I wouldn't say I'm impulsive.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your daily activities or prioritizing tasks outside of work?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? Um... I'm not sure. But... well, I guess that's actually why I made the appointment. It's more at the shop. I just... I can't seem to keep my work in order anymore. \n\nEverything just gets piled together. The tickets, the receipts... workshop notes, customer jobs. It just turns into this big pile and... I don't know what to handle first.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having trouble keeping things organized at work?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's been a while now. I guess... maybe about two years and two months? That's when it really started causing trouble. It was right around the time I started trying to change the shop into, you know, a more complicated boutique model. Things just got harder to keep straight after that.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble paying attention to details, or is this something that has developed over time?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's always been there, really. It's not just recent. \n\nEven back when I was a kid... my teachers used to tell my mother that I was, you know, bright but scattered. I was always talking out of turn, or losing my papers. They were always having to remind me to finish my work. So... yeah. It's been a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I haven't paid much attention to that. I mean... I'm on my feet most of the day at the cleaners anyway, so... I guess I haven't really noticed if I am.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble with relationships at home or with friends because of the problems you're having at work?\n\nPatient: Um... I suppose a little. At the shop, it's just... well, there have been some tense moments lately. You know, with the staff, and some of the customers. Just from things like late orders, or... getting the billing confused. \n\nAnd... I guess outside of work... well, at church, I've actually stepped back from leading some of the planning we do. People were just getting a bit frustrated with me. So... I just thought it was better to step back.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant impact on your daily life outside of work due to your difficulties with organization and focus?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, besides what I already said about church... I don't think there's much else. I just try to keep to myself mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're moving or fidgeting when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. Like I said, I'm standing a lot at the cleaners... so I don't sit still very often. When I do, I... I don't really pay attention to what my hands or feet are doing. So... no, I haven't noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Were you more fidgety or restless as a child?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really remember. It's been a long time since I was a kid. I guess I was just a normal child... I don't really think about it much.\n\nDoctor: Were you ever diagnosed with attention deficit hyperactivity disorder as a child?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not diagnosed. I mean... my teachers back in grade school used to tell my mother I was scattered, I guess. They said I was bright but... I was always talking out of turn, or losing my papers. I needed a lot of reminders to just finish my work. But... no, no doctor ever said anything about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you are in situations where you need to be still, such as in meetings or while watching a movie?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess when I'm in church committee meetings... or sometimes we have these fabric-care trainings for work... I don't really stay in my chair. \n\nI usually end up finding some reason to get up. Like... to get a drink of water, or grab some papers... or just check on something. Even when everybody else is just sitting still, I... I just get up. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Um... sometimes, I guess. At work, mostly. I mean, I supervise the sorting... and if my staff are talking, I might just cut in. Or if they're doing something wrong with the clothes, I just reach over and fix it before they even ask. I know it probably seems like I'm... butting in. But I just do it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]
